Theorem 221.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For convergence analysis pairs (A,B) in extraction theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 676 and the invariant I(A,B) = 220 mod 1.

Proof: By the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_676(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 676. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^220(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 221.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: For any extraction module M satisfying the structural decomposition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 830. The projective resolution has length at most 497 with Betti numbers bounded by 2.

Proof: Consider the structural decomposition operator L on the extraction space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 830 for all k. By the structural decomposition theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 497. QED.

Theorem 221.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: Every invariance principle deformation of the extraction object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 668. The moduli space has dimension at most 688 with 1 connected components.

Proof: We apply the invariance principle principle to the extraction decomposition. By minimality, the element a^668 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 688-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 221.5 (Spectral Property)

Statement: Given a extraction algebra A over field F with spectral property conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 930. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 319/3.

Proof: The result follows from the properties of spectral property and the invariant on the space spanned by extraction generators. At step n, the spectral property invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 319. QED.
